<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture for arbitrary origin-symmetric convex bodies in every dimension. The theorem also gives the symmetric <i>L</i><sub><i>p</i></sub> Brunn–Minkowski inequality for every $`0\lt p\lt 1`$. Combined with Saroglou's transfer theorem and a support-subspace reduction, it yields the logarithmic inequality for every even log-concave Radon measure and the scalar-dilation $`(B)`$-conjecture.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\ge1$ and let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be convex bodies satisfying $K=-K$ and $L=-L$. For every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $$\label{eq:main} \left\|\mathcal W[h_K^{1-\lambda}h_L^\lambda]\right\| \ge \|K\|^{1-\lambda}\|L\|^\lambda.$$
  - Explanation: Geometric interpolation of positive support bounds followed by the Wulff intersection has volume at least the geometric interpolation of the endpoint volumes for origin-symmetric convex bodies.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lp)): Let $n\ge1$, let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be full-dimensional origin-symmetric convex bodies, and let $0<p<1$. For every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $$\label{eq:lp} \left\|\mathcal W\!\left[ \bigl((1-\lambda)h_K^p+\lambda h_L^p\bigr)^{1/p} \right]\right\|^{p/n} \ge (1-\lambda)\|K\|^{p/n}+\lambda\|L\|^{p/n}.$$
  - Explanation: The symmetric volume inequality for the weighted power mean follows from the logarithmic inequality by normalization and reweighting.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-variance)): Let $n\ge2$, $N\ge1$, $q\ge2$ be an even integer, and $\varepsilon>0$. Let $p_1,\ldots,p_N\in\mathbb R^n\setminus\{0\}$ and $h_1,\ldots,h_N>0$. Set $$\label{eq:potential} \psi_i(x)=\left(\frac{\langle p_i,x\rangle}{h_i}\right)^q, \qquad V(x)=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\|x\|^2+\sum_{i=1}^N\psi_i(x), \qquad d\mu=Z^{-1}e^{-V(x)}\,dx,$$ where $Z=\int_{\mathbb R^n}e^{-V(x)}\,dx$. For all real $b_1,\ldots,b_N$, $$\label{eq:variance} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu\left(\sum_{i=1}^N b_i\psi_i\right) \le\sum_{i=1}^N b_i^2\mathbb E_\mu\psi_i.$$
  - Explanation: The variance of a real linear combination of the even slab summands is bounded by the sum of their expectations weighted by the squared coefficients. The positive Gaussian term permits nonspanning directions.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-moment)): Let $n\ge2$, $\varepsilon>0$, and let $V\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ be even with $D^2V\ge\varepsilon I$. Set $$Z=\int_{\mathbb R^n}e^{-V(x)}\,dx, \qquad d\mu(x)=Z^{-1}e^{-V(x)}\,dx.$$ There is an even $\varphi\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ such that $d\nu(z)=e^{-\varphi(z)}\,dz$ is a probability measure, $\nabla\varphi$ is a diffeomorphism of $\mathbb R^n$ onto $\mathbb R^n$, and $$\label{eq:moment} (\nabla\varphi)_\#\nu=\mu, \qquad 0<D^2\varphi\le\frac4\varepsilon I, \qquad \log\det D^2\varphi =V(\nabla\varphi)+\log Z-\varphi.$$ Moreover, $\varphi(z)\ge c\|z\|-b$ for some $c>0$ and $b<\infty$. The lower Hessian bound in <a href="#eq:moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:moment">[eq:moment]</a> means positive definiteness at every point; it need not be uniform on $\mathbb R^n$.
  - Explanation: An even uniformly convex potential admits smooth moment coordinates with a global upper Hessian bound, pointwise strict positivity, and a coercive moment potential. A uniform positive lower Hessian bound is not asserted.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-density)): Let $d\mu=\rho(x)\,dx$ be a probability measure on $\mathbb R^n$ with a smooth, strictly positive, even density. Let $\tau$ be a smooth even symmetric matrix field with $0<\tau(x)\le\Lambda I$ for some finite $\Lambda$, and set $$Af=x\cdot\nabla f-\tau:D^2f.$$ Suppose that $A$ has the integration formula $$\mathbb E[hAf]=\mathbb E[\nabla h\cdot\tau\nabla f] \qquad(f,h\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n))$$ and satisfies the compact Hessian identity <a href="#eq:hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hessian">[eq:hessian]</a>. Then $$\overline{\{A(A-1)f:f\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n),\ f\text{ even}\}}^{\,L^2(\mu)} =\{g\in L^2(\mu):g\text{ even},\ \mathbb Eg=0\}.$$
  - Explanation: The double-divergence tests are dense among centered even square-integrable functions when the stated integration and Hessian identities hold. Centering and evenness remove the affine obstructions.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor)): Let $d\mu=Z^{-1}e^{-V}dx$ and $d\nu=e^{-\varphi}dz$ be smooth probability measures on $\mathbb R^n$. Suppose $D_z^2\varphi$ is positive definite and $x=\nabla\varphi(z)$ is a smooth diffeomorphism pushing $\nu$ to $\mu$. For a real $f\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R_x^n)$, put $$\tau=D_z^2\varphi,\quad M=\tau^{-1},\quad H=D_x^2V, \quad B=\tau(D_x^2f)\tau, \quad W=\delta_\mu B, \quad u=\delta_\mu W.$$ View source fields in target coordinates by the inverse moment map. Then $$\label{eq:tensor-estimate} \mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl((D_xW)^2\bigr) \ge \mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(MBHB),$$ and $$\label{eq:tensor-energy} \mathbb Eu^2\ge\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(MBHB)+\mathbb EW^THW.$$ Here $(D_xW)_{ij}=\partial_{x_j}W_i$, and the square in <a href="#eq:tensor-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tensor-estimate">[eq:tensor-estimate]</a> is a matrix square.
  - Explanation: The moment-coordinate tensor calculation bounds the matrix-square trace and the double-divergence energy. It applies to fields built from a compactly supported scalar test under the stated moment-map hypotheses.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-measure)): Let $n\ge1$, let $\mu$ be an even log-concave Radon measure on $\mathbb R^n$, and let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be origin-symmetric convex bodies. Then, for every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $$\label{eq:measure-logbm} \mu\bigl(\mathcal W[h_K^{1-\lambda}h_L^\lambda]\bigr) \ge \mu(K)^{1-\lambda}\mu(L)^\lambda.$$ Consequently, for every origin-symmetric convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, the function $$\label{eq:b-function} \mathbb R\ni t\longmapsto \mu(e^tK)$$ is log-concave.
  - Explanation: The Wulff measure inequality holds for even log-concave Radon measures, including measures supported on proper subspaces. Applying it to scalar dilates gives log-concavity of their measured mass.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Convex body** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A convex body in $\mathbb R^n$ is a compact convex set with nonempty interior.
- **Support function** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its support function is $$h_K(u)=\max_{x\in K}\langle x,u\rangle,\qquad u\in S^{n-1}.$$
- **Wulff body** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a positive continuous function $f$ on the unit sphere, its *Wulff body* is $$\mathcal W[f]=\bigcap_{u\in S^{n-1}} \{x\in\mathbb R^n:\langle x,u\rangle\le f(u)\}.$$
- **Lebesgue volume** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\|K\|$ for $n$-dimensional Lebesgue measure.
- **Expectation and variance** ([source](#sec:reduction)): For a probability measure $\mu$, write $\mathbb E_\mu f=\int f\,d\mu$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu(f)=\mathbb E_\mu(f-\mathbb E_\mu f)^2$.
- **Even log-concave density** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An even log-concave density has the form $e^{-V}$, where $V$ is an even convex function with values in $\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$; no probability normalization is required.
- **Negative weighted divergence** ([source](#sec:identities)): In target coordinates, write $\delta_\mu$ for negative weighted divergence: $$\delta_\mu W =\sum_j\bigl(V_{x_j}W_j-\partial_{x_j}W_j\bigr).$$
- **Double-divergence test fields** ([source](#eq:test-fields)): For $f\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R_x^n)$, define $$\label{eq:test-fields} h=D_x^2f,\qquad P=\tau h,\qquad B=\tau h\tau, \qquad W_a=\partial_k^*P_{ak},\qquad u=\delta_\mu W.$$
- **Log-concave and even Radon measure** ([source](#sec:measure)): A nonnegative Radon measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$ is *log-concave* if $$\mu((1-\theta)A+\theta B) \ge \mu(A)^{1-\theta}\mu(B)^\theta \qquad(0<\theta<1)$$ for all compact sets $A,B\subset\mathbb R^n$; it is *even* if $\mu(A)=\mu(-A)$ for every Borel set $A$.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Ambient dimension | ≥ | $1$ | Positive integer dimension. |
| Q2 | Even slab degree | ≥ | $2$ | Even integer degree. |
| Q3 | Gaussian regularization parameter | > | $0$ | Strictly positive regularization. |
| Q4 | Moment Hessian upper cap | ≤ | $\frac4\varepsilon I$ | Uniform upper cap; the positive lower bound is pointwise. |
| Q5 | Normalized endpoint volume | = | $1$ | Normalization in the interior interpolation case. |
| Q6 | Antisymmetric tensor-square coefficient | = | $\frac16$ | Fully symmetric derivative tensor and a tensor symmetric in its last indices. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moment): Compact moment potentials, uniform Hessian caps, coercive domination, interior regularity and a diagonal smooth limit. -> Whole-space moment diffeomorphism with bounded positive Hessian.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-density) / [proof](#sec:identities): Weighted adjunction and compact integration by parts. -> Double-divergence representation and compact Hessian identity.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density): Elliptic regularity, cutoff estimates and characterization of the affine kernel. -> Density of compact even double-divergence tests.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor): Weighted Bochner identity and invariant tensor contractions in constant normalized coordinates, followed by an explicit sum of squares. -> Tensor energy lower bound.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-variance) / [proof](#sec:variance): Homogeneity, complementary matrix and vector Cauchy–Schwarz bounds, and extension by density. -> Summandwise variance bound.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-variance): Partition-function concavity, dominated convergence of even slab degrees, monotone removal of regularization, and continuity from above of dense slab approximations. -> Logarithmic Wulff volume inequality.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-cor-lp) / [proof](#subsec:lp-proof): Normalize endpoint volumes, reweight the parameter and apply weighted arithmetic–geometric mean, Wulff monotonicity and homogeneity. -> Additive symmetric volume-power inequality.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-cor-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-measure): Restrict to a symmetric compact ball, reduce to the supporting linear subspace, use the even density representation and transfer theorem, then apply scalar dilations. -> Measure inequality and scalar-dilation log-concavity.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **symmetric_wulff**: The volume lower bound applies to the Wulff body formed from geometric interpolation of origin-symmetric support bounds.
- **tensor_remainder**: The normalized tensor remainder is a sum of nonnegative squares under the stated tensor symmetries.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **omit_wulff**: Treat the geometric mean of support bounds as a support function. -> The interpolated bounds need not define a support function directly.
- **finite_fullproof**: Use a finite tensor check as certification of the volume theorem. -> The analytic moment coordinates, density and limiting passages remain unchecked.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The explicit normalized tensor remainder has a finite sum-of-squares evaluation. This checks that algebraic component; the moment-map construction, density argument and limiting volume theorem remain outside the checked scope.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of rational tensors S and J, each with n^3 entries.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 2; S fully symmetric; J symmetric in its last two indices; at most 1024 cases.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact rational tensor square energies as strings.
# TIME: O(batch_size * n^3) rational operations; bit complexity depends on input size.
# SPACE: O(batch_size * n^3) rational entries.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq, exact rational operations, no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid dimensions, symmetry or rational entries raise an exception.
import json, sys
from itertools import product, permutations
from flint import fmpq
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert 1 <= len(cases) <= 1024
answer = []
for case in cases:
    n = case['n']
    assert type(n) is int and 1 <= n <= 2
    assert len(case['s']) == len(case['j']) == n**3
    s = list(map(fmpq, case['s']))
    j = list(map(fmpq, case['j']))
    def pos(a,b,c): return (a*n+b)*n+c
    energy = fmpq(0)
    for a,b,c in product(range(n), repeat=3):
        k = pos(a,b,c)
        assert all(s[k] == s[pos(*p)] for p in permutations((a,b,c)))
        assert j[k] == j[pos(a,c,b)]
        sym = (j[k]+j[pos(b,a,c)]+j[pos(c,a,b)])/3
        skew = j[k]-j[pos(b,a,c)]
        energy += 2*(s[k]+sym)**2 + fmpq(1,6)*skew**2
    assert energy >= 0
    answer.append(str(energy))
print(json.dumps(answer))
```

```json
    "output_contract": "Exact nonnegative rational tensor remainder for each admitted case.",
    "reason": "The explicit normalized tensor remainder has a finite sum-of-squares evaluation. This checks that algebraic component; the moment-map construction, density argument and limiting volume theorem remain outside the checked scope.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON batch of rational tensors S and J, each with n^3 entries.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 2; S fully symmetric; J symmetric in its last two indices; at most 1024 cases.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact rational tensor square energies as strings.\n# TIME: O(batch_size * n^3) rational operations; bit complexity depends on input size.\n# SPACE: O(batch_size * n^3) rational entries.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq, exact rational operations, no floating point.\n# FAILURE: Invalid dimensions, symmetry or rational entries raise an exception.\nimport json, sys\nfrom itertools import product, permutations\nfrom flint import fmpq\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert 1 <= len(cases) <= 1024\nanswer = []\nfor case in cases:\n    n = case['n']\n    assert type(n) is int and 1 <= n <= 2\n    assert len(case['s']) == len(case['j']) == n**3\n    s = list(map(fmpq, case['s']))\n    j = list(map(fmpq, case['j']))\n    def pos(a,b,c): return (a*n+b)*n+c\n    energy = fmpq(0)\n    for a,b,c in product(range(n), repeat=3):\n        k = pos(a,b,c)\n        assert all(s[k] == s[pos(*p)] for p in permutations((a,b,c)))\n        assert j[k] == j[pos(a,c,b)]\n        sym = (j[k]+j[pos(b,a,c)]+j[pos(c,a,b)])/3\n        skew = j[k]-j[pos(b,a,c)]\n        energy += 2*(s[k]+sym)**2 + fmpq(1,6)*skew**2\n    assert energy >= 0\n    answer.append(str(energy))\nprint(json.dumps(answer))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "[\"0\", \"0\", \"2\"]",
        "input": "[{\"n\": 1, \"s\": [\"0\"], \"j\": [\"0\"]}, {\"n\": 1, \"s\": [\"1\"], \"j\": [\"-1\"]}, {\"n\": 1, \"s\": [\"1\"], \"j\": [\"0\"]}]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[\"0\"]",
        "input": "[{\"n\": 2, \"s\": [\"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\"], \"j\": [\"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\", \"0\"]}]"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[\"88/9\"]",
        "input": "[{\"n\": 2, \"s\": [\"-4/3\", \"-1\", \"-1\", \"-2/3\", \"-1\", \"-2/3\", \"-2/3\", \"-1/3\"], \"j\": [\"0\", \"1/3\", \"1/3\", \"2/3\", \"1\", \"4/3\", \"4/3\", \"5/3\"]}]"
      },
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Inferred conditional use: a geometry application whose inputs satisfy the theorem may use the logarithmic volume inequality as a mathematical consistency requirement. Software construction and certified numerical evaluation are ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper supplies no dynamics, collision method or simulation contract; its geometric inequalities alone do not establish simulation accuracy. |
| tools | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Inferred conditional use: a geometric validation tool may compare its independently certified volume bounds with the theorem under the exact symmetry and Wulff hypotheses. A volume-computation tool and approximation tolerance are ABSENT. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the proof passes through smooth integrals, moment coordinates and limiting slab approximations without a finite algorithm with certified stopping or error bounds. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: finite slab constraints describe mathematical sets, but the paper gives no storage interface, update procedure or data-structure complexity. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives no hardware architecture, parallel kernel or accelerator performance contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives no cryptographic primitive, adversary model or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Inferred conditional use: analysis of a probabilistic model with the exact soft-slab potential may use the variance bound for linear combinations of its homogeneous summands. Training, estimation and generalisation guarantees are ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Inferred conditional use: a model with a volume objective along geometrically interpolated support bounds may use the stated logarithmic lower bound. No optimisation solver, convergence rate or globally valid parameterisation is supplied. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives no communication, replication or distributed agreement contract. |
| other (convex geometry and log-concave measure analysis) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper directly proves logarithmic and intermediate power-mean volume inequalities for origin-symmetric convex bodies, a summandwise variance bound, and the measure inequality with scalar-dilation log-concavity. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-symmetry** (hypothesis): The logarithmic volume inequality requires origin-symmetric convex bodies in the same ambient space. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated theorem to a body without origin symmetry.']
- **limit-wulff** (approximation): Geometrically interpolated support bounds must be intersected through the Wulff construction; the interpolated function need not be a support function. Blocked use: ['Replacing the Wulff intersection by an assumed support function.']
- **limit-lp** (hypothesis): The stated intermediate power-mean volume inequality requires full-dimensional origin-symmetric bodies and the stated open exponent range. Blocked use: ['Extending the corollary to bodies without full dimensionality or to exponents outside its stated range.']
- **limit-variance** (hypothesis): The summandwise variance bound uses even powers, positive slab widths, nonzero directions, and a positive Gaussian perturbation. Blocked use: ['Applying this bound to arbitrary summands, odd powers, zero directions, or an unregularized potential without a separate justification.']
- **limit-spanning** (hypothesis): Spanning directions are required to make the finite slab polytope bounded, although the Gaussian-regularized variance estimate does not require spanning. Blocked use: ['Using a nonspanning finite slab intersection as a bounded volume approximation.']
- **limit-limits** (approximation): The volume reduction first sends the even power to infinity with the Gaussian parameter fixed, and subsequently removes the Gaussian parameter. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite soft-slab integral as the exact polytope volume or claiming a finite approximation error from these limiting arguments.']
- **limit-hessian** (hypothesis): Moment Hessian positivity is pointwise and does not assert a uniform positive lower bound on the whole space. Blocked use: ['Assuming global uniform conditioning of the moment Hessian or its inverse.']
- **limit-existence** (nonconstructive): The moment-coordinate construction obtains a smooth potential by compactness and a diagonal subsequence; it does not provide a finite implementation with a certified stopping rule. Blocked use: ['Treating existence of the moment map as a supplied finite algorithm.']
- **limit-operator** (formal_scope): The operator formulas are formal integration identities for compact smooth tests and do not assert an operator domain. Blocked use: ['Reading the displayed differential expression as a specified self-adjoint operator realization.']
- **limit-density** (hypothesis): The density conclusion concerns centered even functions under a smooth positive even probability density and a bounded positive definite matrix field satisfying the integration and Hessian identities. Blocked use: ['Using the density conclusion without these identities, parity, or centering.']
- **limit-trace** (formal_scope): The tensor estimate retains the trace of a matrix square; it must not be silently replaced by the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the target-coordinate derivative. Blocked use: ['Replacing the trace contraction by a different matrix norm in the tensor inequality.']
- **limit-compact** (hypothesis): The tensor calculations are stated for compactly supported smooth test functions and use compactly supported fields in integration by parts. Blocked use: ['Applying the identities to noncompact tests without proving decay and integrability.']
- **limit-dilation** (counterexample_scope): The consequence for general even log-concave measures concerns scalar dilations; the diagonal statement discussed here is restricted to uniform measures on cubes. Blocked use: ['Claiming the diagonal dilation property for every even log-concave measure from this consequence.']
- **limit-radon** (edge_case): The measure inequality allows zero measures and measures supported on proper subspaces; total mass need not be finite. Blocked use: ['Requiring an ambient positive probability density for every measure covered by the corollary.']
- **limit-relative** (formal_scope): The relative Wulff inclusion does not assert strict containment. Blocked use: ['Claiming strict containment in every support-subspace reduction.']
- **limit-equality** (edge_case): The logarithmic volume inequality is equality in dimension one and at interpolation endpoints in every dimension. Blocked use: ['Claiming strict improvement in every admissible case.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Scope note of family 091 (`lean/docs/091.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture](../../preprints/The-logarithmic-Brunn-Minkowski-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture asserts that logarithmic interpolation of origin-symmetric convex bodies preserves the geometric-mean lower bound for volume. The formalized result proves, for every dimension $n\ge1$, such bodies $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$, and $0\le t\le1$, that their logarithmic Wulff combination has volume at least $|K|^{1-t}|L|^t$. It assumes neither boundary smoothness nor coordinatewise unconditionality.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open Set MeasureTheory
open scoped ENNReal InnerProductSpace

namespace LogBrunnMinkowski

abbrev Space (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)

def IsConvexBody {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  IsCompact K ∧ Convex ℝ K ∧ (interior K).Nonempty

def OriginSymmetric {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, x ∈ K ↔ -x ∈ K

def support {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) (u : Space n) : ℝ :=
  sSup ((fun x : Space n => ⟪x, u⟫_ℝ) '' K)

def wulff {n : ℕ} (f : Space n → ℝ) : Set (Space n) :=
  {x | ∀ u, ‖u‖ = 1 → ⟪x, u⟫_ℝ ≤ f u}

def logCombination {n : ℕ} (K L : Set (Space n)) (t : ℝ) :
    Set (Space n) :=
  wulff (fun u => (support K u) ^ (1 - t) * (support L u) ^ t)

theorem main (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) (K L : Set (Space n))
    (hK : IsConvexBody K) (hL : IsConvexBody L)
    (hsK : OriginSymmetric K) (hsL : OriginSymmetric L)
    (t : ℝ) (ht0 : 0 ≤ t) (ht1 : t ≤ 1) :
    (volume K) ^ (1 - t) * (volume L) ^ t ≤ volume (logCombination K L t) := by
  sorry

end LogBrunnMinkowski

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture for arbitrary origin-symmetric convex bodies in every dimension. The theorem also gives the symmetric <i>L</i><sub><i>p</i></sub> Brunn–Minkowski inequality for every $`0\lt p\lt 1`$. Combined with Saroglou's transfer theorem and a support-subspace reduction, it yields the logarithmic inequality for every even log-concave Radon measure and the scalar-dilation $`(B)`$-conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| [stmt-cor-lp](#stmt-cor-lp) | corollary | `cor:lp` | 2 | Let $n\ge1$, let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be full-dimensional origin-symmetric convex bodies, and let $0<p<1$. |
| [stmt-prop-variance](#stmt-prop-variance) | proposition | `prop:variance` | 3 | Let $n\ge2$, $N\ge1$, $q\ge2$ be an even integer, and $\varepsilon>0$. |
| [stmt-lem-moment](#stmt-lem-moment) | lemma | `lem:moment` | 4 | Let $n\ge2$, $\varepsilon>0$, and let $V\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ be even with $D^2V\ge\varepsilon I$. |

| [stmt-cor-measure](#stmt-cor-measure) | corollary | `cor:measure` | 7 | $-conjecture). *Let $n\ge1$, let $\mu$ be an even log-concave Radon measure on $\mathbb R^n$, and let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be origin-symmetric convex bodies. Then, for every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $ |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A convex body in $\mathbb R^n$ is a compact convex set with nonempty interior. If $K=-K$, then $0$ lies in its interior. Its support function is $$h_K(u)=\max_{x\in K}\langle x,u\rangle,\qquad u\in S^{n-1}.$$ For an origin-symmetric body this function is strictly positive. For a positive continuous function $f$ on the unit sphere, its *Wulff body* is $$\mathcal W[f]=\bigcap_{u\in S^{n-1}}
 \{x\in\mathbb R^n:\langle x,u\rangle\le f(u)\}.$$ It is a convex body: it contains the ball of radius $\min f>0$ and is contained in the ball of radius $\max f$. Write $|K|$ for $n$-dimensional Lebesgue measure. Our main result is the following.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Even logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski inequality). *Let $n\ge1$ and let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be convex bodies satisfying $K=-K$ and $L=-L$. For every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $$\label{eq:main}
 \left|\mathcal W[h_K^{1-\lambda}h_L^\lambda]\right|
 \ge |K|^{1-\lambda}|L|^\lambda.$$*

</div>

The interpolation in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a> takes the geometric mean of the support bounds before intersecting the half-spaces. This gives a strengthening, in the symmetric setting, of the multiplicative form of the classical Brunn–Minkowski inequality: the arithmetic–geometric mean inequality gives $$\mathcal W[h_K^{1-\lambda}h_L^\lambda]
 \subset (1-\lambda)K+\lambda L.$$ In general $h_K^{1-\lambda}h_L^\lambda$ need not itself be a support function. The intersection defining $\mathcal W$ is essential throughout the argument.

For $0<p<1$, the $L_p$ interpolation replaces the geometric mean of the support bounds by the weighted power mean $((1-\lambda)h_K^p+\lambda h_L^p)^{1/p}$, still taking the Wulff body. The logarithmic endpoint implies the full symmetric volume inequality in this intermediate range. Böröczky, Lutwak, Yang and Zhang record this monotone implication (Böröczky et al. 2012, equation (1.10) and the following paragraph, p. 1977) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BLZY2012](#ref-BLZY2012)]. The additive volume-power form below follows by normalizing the bodies and reweighting the interpolation parameter.

<a id="stmt-cor-lp"></a>
<div id="cor:lp" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:lp"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Symmetric $L_p$ Brunn–Minkowski inequality). *Let $n\ge1$, let $K,L\subset\mathbb R^n$ be full-dimensional origin-symmetric convex bodies, and let $0<p<1$. For every $0\le\lambda\le1$, $$\label{eq:lp}
 \left|\mathcal W\!\left[
  \bigl((1-\lambda)h_K^p+\lambda h_L^p\bigr)^{1/p}
 \right]\right|^{p/n}
 \ge (1-\lambda)|K|^{p/n}+\lambda|L|^{p/n}.$$*

</div>

The proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:lp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:lp">2</a> is given in Section <a href="#subsec:lp-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:lp-proof">8.1</a>.

The logarithmic volume theorem has a separate measure-theoretic consequence. An even log-concave density has the form $e^{-V}$, where $V$ is an even convex function with values in $\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$; no probability normalization is required. Saroglou proved that the Lebesgue inequality in dimension $n$ implies the same Wulff inequality for every such density in that same dimension (Saroglou 2016, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Saroglou2016](#ref-Saroglou2016)]. Taking two scalar dilates of a single body then gives the $(B)$-conjecture: for the measure $d\mu=e^{-V(x)}\,dx$ and every origin-symmetric convex body $K$, the function $t\mapsto\mu(e^tK)$ is log-concave on $\mathbb R$ (Saroglou 2016, Corollary 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Saroglou2016](#ref-Saroglou2016)]. Section <a href="#sec:measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:measure">8.2</a> applies this transfer and also treats even log-concave Radon measures supported on proper subspaces.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and prior work

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the origin-symmetric logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture of Böröczky, Lutwak, Yang and Zhang (Böröczky et al. 2012, Problem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BLZY2012](#ref-BLZY2012)]. Stancu (Stancu 2018, Theorem 2, p. 3264) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Stancu2018](#ref-Stancu2018)] announced the all-dimensional logarithmic Minkowski inequality and explicitly concluded the equivalent logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski inequality; the report deferred details of the flow asymptotics. The conjecture belongs to the $L_p$ Brunn–Minkowski theory initiated by Firey’s $p$-means of convex bodies (Firey 1962) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Firey1962](#ref-Firey1962)] and developed by Lutwak through $L_p$ mixed volumes and the $L_p$ Minkowski problem (Lutwak 1993) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Lutwak1993](#ref-Lutwak1993)]. For $p>0$, the support bounds are interpolated by the weighted power mean appearing in Corollary <a href="#cor:lp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:lp">2</a>; the geometric mean is its limit as $p\downarrow0$. This endpoint has a particularly close connection with cone-volume measures and the logarithmic Minkowski problem. The cone-volume measure records the volumes of cones from the origin to boundary patches, indexed by their outer normals. Böröczky, Lutwak, Yang and Zhang established the equivalence between the corresponding logarithmic volume and mixed-volume inequalities (Böröczky et al. 2012, Lemma 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BLZY2012](#ref-BLZY2012)]; their subsequent work characterized which even measures arise as cone-volume measures (Böröczky et al. 2013, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BLYZ2013](#ref-BLYZ2013)]. The latter is an existence result, separate from the volume inequality proved here.

Several important classes were established in earlier work. Böröczky, Lutwak, Yang and Zhang proved the planar case (Böröczky et al. 2012, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BLZY2012](#ref-BLZY2012)]. Saroglou established the inequality and studied its equality cases for bodies unconditional with respect to the same orthonormal basis, meaning that both bodies are invariant under every coordinate sign change in that basis (Saroglou 2015, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Saroglou2015](#ref-Saroglou2015)]. The underlying volume inequality for coordinatewise products has antecedents in Uhrin, Bollobás–Leader, and Cordero-Erausquin–Fradelizi–Maurey (Uhrin 1994; Bollobás and Leader 1995; D. Cordero-Erausquin, Fradelizi, and Maurey 2004) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Uhrin1994](#ref-Uhrin1994), [BollobasLeader1995](#ref-BollobasLeader1995), [CFM2004](#ref-CFM2004)]; Saroglou connected this product to the logarithmic combination. This argument uses the multiplicative form of the Prékopa–Leindler inequality. Böröczky and Kalantzopoulos extended the symmetry method to bodies invariant under the same $n$ linear reflections whose fixed hyperplanes intersect only at the origin (Böröczky and Kalantzopoulos 2022, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BK2022](#ref-BK2022)]. For unit balls of complex norms, Rotem derived the inequality from Cordero-Erausquin’s volume inequality for complex interpolation (Rotem 2014; Dario Cordero-Erausquin 2002) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Rotem2014](#ref-Rotem2014), [Cordero2002](#ref-Cordero2002)].

A complementary approach studies the second variation of volume. Colesanti, Livshyts and Marsiglietti established a local result near Euclidean balls and developed infinitesimal formulations (Colesanti, Livshyts, and Marsiglietti 2017) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CLM2017](#ref-CLM2017)]. Kolesnikov and Milman expressed the local $L_p$ inequality as a lower bound for the first nonconstant even eigenvalue of the Hilbert–Brunn–Minkowski operator, and proved it for $p\ge 1-c n^{-3/2}$ with a universal $c>0$ (A. V. Kolesnikov and Milman 2022) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[KM2022](#ref-KM2022)]. Their spectral formulation explains why symmetry matters: the translation eigenmodes are odd and hence disappear in the even subspace. Chen, Huang, Li and Liu obtained the corresponding global inequalities for $p$ sufficiently close to $1$ by a PDE argument (Chen et al. 2020) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CHLL2020](#ref-CHLL2020)]. Putterman proved the equivalence of local and global $L_p$ inequalities for every $p\in[0,1)$ by studying strongly isomorphic polytopes (Putterman 2021, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Putterman2021](#ref-Putterman2021)]. Van Handel proved the local logarithmic inequality for origin-symmetric zonoids, that is, Hausdorff limits of sums of segments, against arbitrary origin-symmetric comparison bodies (van Handel 2023, Theorem 1.4 of the cited arXiv version) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[vanHandel2023](#ref-vanHandel2023)]. Milman’s centro-affine interpretation of the operator led to further curvature-dependent results and an isomorphic logarithmic Minkowski theorem: every symmetric body has a suitable replacement within a universal geometric factor (Milman 2025) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Milman2025](#ref-Milman2025)]. These developments identify the analytic obstruction without imposing a common normal fan on the global conjecture.

Iffland gave another spectral proof of the local logarithmic inequality for origin-symmetric bodies of revolution and extended it to nonsymmetric comparison bodies under a weighted centering condition (Iffland 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Iffland2026](#ref-Iffland2026)]. Lu proved the global inequality for origin-symmetric pairs sharing $n-2$ orthogonal reflection symmetries (Lu 2026, Theorems 1.2 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Lu2026](#ref-Lu2026)]. Xi (Xi 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Xi2026](#ref-Xi2026)] gives a new planar proof that also treats Dar’s conjecture. These results illustrate the different roles of local variation and additional symmetries in the problem.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

For finitely many spanning unit vectors $p_i$ and positive numbers $h_i$, the constraints $|\langle p_i,x\rangle|\le h_i$ define a symmetric polytope. Replace its indicator by the smooth weight $$\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|x|^2
       -\sum_i\left(\frac{\langle p_i,x\rangle}{h_i}\right)^q\right),
 \qquad \varepsilon>0,\quad q\ge2\text{ even}.$$ As $q$ tends to infinity, this weight becomes the Gaussian weight restricted to the polytope. Letting $\varepsilon$ tend to zero recovers its volume. Thus it suffices to prove that the integral of the smooth weight is log-concave when each $h_i$ varies geometrically. Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">2</a> makes this reduction precise, including the passage from finitely many directions to all directions.

Geometric changes of slab widths are also related to the $(B)$-property, which asks for log-concavity of measure under exponential dilations. Saroglou showed that the diagonal $(B)$-property for uniform measures on cubes in every dimension is equivalent to the logarithmic Brunn–Minkowski conjecture in every dimension (Saroglou 2015, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Saroglou2015](#ref-Saroglou2015)]. This relates the geometric reduction to an earlier measure-theoretic formulation. The diagonal statement here is restricted to uniform measures on cubes; the consequence for general even log-concave measures concerns scalar dilations. The summandwise variance estimate below supplies the required concavity directly.

The second derivative of the logarithm of that integral is a variance minus an expected second derivative. The analytic task is therefore a variance bound for sums of even powers of linear forms (Proposition <a href="#prop:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:variance">3</a>). We prove it in moment coordinates: the probability with density proportional to the displayed weight is the image of $e^{-\varphi(z)}\,dz$ under $x=\nabla\varphi(z)$. Section <a href="#sec:moment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:moment">3</a> constructs these coordinates with a bounded, everywhere positive Hessian, starting from the compact moment theorem of Berman and Berndtsson (Berman and Berndtsson 2013) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[BermanBerndtsson2013](#ref-BermanBerndtsson2013)] and Klartag’s Hessian estimate (Klartag 2013) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[KlartagArxiv2013](#ref-KlartagArxiv2013)]. The general moment-measure theory is due to Cordero-Erausquin and Klartag (Dario Cordero-Erausquin and Klartag 2015) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CEK2015](#ref-CEK2015)].

Two steps complete the analytic argument. First, a differential operator associated with the moment Hessian produces a dense class of centered even test functions (Sections <a href="#sec:identities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:identities">4</a> and <a href="#sec:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:density">5</a>). The only obstructions to density are affine functions: centering removes constants, and evenness removes linear functions. Second, a tensor calculation for these tests has a remainder that is the sum of explicit squares (Section <a href="#sec:tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensor">6</a>). This supplies the variance bound by two Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities (Section <a href="#sec:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:variance">7</a>).

The analytic setting belongs to the tradition of Brascamp–Lieb variance inequalities for log-concave measures (Brascamp and Lieb 1976) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BrascampLieb1976](#ref-BrascampLieb1976)]. Kolesnikov, Livshyts and Rotem (A. Kolesnikov, Livshyts, and Rotem 2026, Theorem 1.4 and Corollaries 1.5–1.6) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[KLR2026](#ref-KLR2026)] relate strengthened Brascamp–Lieb inequalities for homogeneous density potentials to local $p$-Brunn–Minkowski inequalities and obtain the local logarithmic inequality for $\ell_q$ balls, $q\ge1$. Their homogeneous variance formulation uses the Hessian of the density potential; our summandwise estimate uses the separately constructed moment potential. The transport-Hessian diffusion and curvature formulas were studied earlier by Kolesnikov (A. V. Kolesnikov 2014) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Kolesnikov2014](#ref-Kolesnikov2014)]; the Bochner identities in (A. Kolesnikov, Livshyts, and Rotem 2026, sec. 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[KLR2026](#ref-KLR2026)] build on this framework.

The ingredient here is a tensor estimate in moment coordinates that controls each homogeneous summand separately. The accompanying density argument uses a global upper Hessian bound and local strict positivity; it does not require a global lower Hessian bound. We state these two steps separately so that their hypotheses and their possible use for other variance estimates are explicit.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### From a variance bound to volume

We first deduce Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> from the variance bound below. For a probability measure $\mu$, write $\mathbb E_\mu f=\int f\,d\mu$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu(f)=\mathbb E_\mu(f-\mathbb E_\mu f)^2$.

<a id="stmt-prop-variance"></a>
<div id="prop:variance" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:potential"></a>
<a id="eq:variance"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Variance bound). *Let $n\ge2$, $N\ge1$, $q\ge2$ be an even integer, and $\varepsilon>0$. Let $p_1,\ldots,p_N\in\mathbb R^n\setminus\{0\}$ and $h_1,\ldots,h_N>0$. Set $$\label{eq:potential}
 \psi_i(x)=\left(\frac{\langle p_i,x\rangle}{h_i}\right)^q,
 \qquad V(x)=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|x|^2+\sum_{i=1}^N\psi_i(x),
 \qquad d\mu=Z^{-1}e^{-V(x)}\,dx,$$ where $Z=\int_{\mathbb R^n}e^{-V(x)}\,dx$. For all real $b_1,\ldots,b_N$, $$\label{eq:variance}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu\left(\sum_{i=1}^N b_i\psi_i\right)
 \le\sum_{i=1}^N b_i^2\mathbb E_\mu\psi_i.$$*

</div>

Here the Gaussian term makes $Z$ finite, even if the vectors $p_i$ do not span $\mathbb R^n$. All polynomial moments are finite. The proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:variance">3</a> occupies Sections <a href="#sec:moment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:moment">3</a>–<a href="#sec:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:variance">7</a>.

The proof will establish the dual estimate $$\sum_{i=1}^N\frac{(\mathbb E_\mu u\psi_i)^2}{\mathbb E_\mu\psi_i}
 \le \mathbb E_\mu u^2
 \qquad\text{for every centered even }u\in L^2(\mu).$$ Here centered means $\mathbb E_\mu u=0$, and the denominators are positive because each $p_i\ne0$ and $\mu$ has a positive density. Applied to $u=\sum_i b_i\psi_i-\mathbb E_\mu\sum_i b_i\psi_i$, this gives <a href="#eq:variance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:variance">[eq:variance]</a> by Cauchy–Schwarz. We first prove the dual estimate on a class of smooth compactly supported tests, then use density to reach every such $u$. This explains the roles of the density and tensor lemmas in the analytic proof.

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-variance"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-variance</summary>

<a id="eq:partition-concavity"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> from Proposition <a href="#prop:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:variance">3</a>.* Suppose first that $n\ge2$. Fix nonzero spanning vectors $p_1,\ldots,p_N$ and positive endpoint widths $h_i^0,h_i^1$. For $t\in[0,1]$, define $$h_i(t)=(h_i^0)^{1-t}(h_i^1)^t,\qquad
 P(t)=\{x:|\langle p_i,x\rangle|\le h_i(t)\text{ for all }i\}.$$ The spanning condition makes each $P(t)$ bounded; positivity of the widths gives nonempty interior. Use these widths in <a href="#eq:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:potential">[eq:potential]</a> and denote the resulting integral by $Z_{q,\varepsilon}(t)$. For fixed $q,\varepsilon$, its first two derivatives may be taken under the integral: on a compact interval of $t$, the differentiated integrands are bounded by a polynomial times $e^{-\varepsilon|x|^2/2}$. With $b_i=-q\log(h_i^1/h_i^0)$ one has $$\dot V=\sum_i b_i\psi_i,\qquad
 \ddot V=\sum_i b_i^2\psi_i,$$ and hence $$\frac{d^2}{dt^2}\log Z_{q,\varepsilon}(t)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mu_t}(\dot V)-\mathbb E_{\mu_t}\ddot V\le0$$ by Proposition <a href="#prop:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:variance">3</a>. Therefore $$\label{eq:partition-concavity}
 Z_{q,\varepsilon}(\lambda)\ge
 Z_{q,\varepsilon}(0)^{1-\lambda}Z_{q,\varepsilon}(1)^\lambda.$$

<a id="eq:slabs"></a>
Keep $\varepsilon>0$ fixed and let $q$ tend to infinity through even integers. For each fixed $t$, the integrands converge almost everywhere to $e^{-\varepsilon|x|^2/2}\mathbf1_{P(t)}$. The exceptional set lies in the finitely many hyperplanes $\langle p_i,x\rangle=\pm h_i(t)$ and has measure zero. The common integrable bound $e^{-\varepsilon|x|^2/2}$ gives $$Z_{q,\varepsilon}(t)\longrightarrow
 \int_{P(t)}e^{-\varepsilon|x|^2/2}\,dx.$$ Apply this at $t=0,\lambda,1$ in <a href="#eq:partition-concavity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:partition-concavity">[eq:partition-concavity]</a>, then let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. Monotone convergence gives $$\label{eq:slabs}
 |P(\lambda)|\ge |P(0)|^{1-\lambda}|P(1)|^\lambda.$$

<figure id="fig:slab-exhaustion">

<figcaption>Finite outer approximations of an ellipse $E$. Adding slab directions gives $P_{2} ⊃ P_{4} ⊃ E$; the subscripts here count slab directions. Using nested finite spanning direction sets with dense union, the same construction recovers $𝒲[f]$ from any positive continuous even function $f$ on the sphere; $f$ need not be a support function.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-slab-exhaustion"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:slab-exhaustion`)

TikZ/PGF source, copied verbatim from the source:

```latex
\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,line join=round]
    \definecolor{slabblue}{RGB}{34,100,139}
    \definecolor{slabfill}{RGB}{222,237,245}
    \begin{scope}[xshift=-2.8cm]
      \filldraw[fill=slabfill,draw=slabblue,line width=0.8pt]
        (-1.6,-1) rectangle (1.6,1);
      \filldraw[fill=white,draw=black,line width=0.6pt]
        (0,0) ellipse [x radius=1.6,y radius=1];
      \node at (0,0) {$E$};
      \node[anchor=north] at (0,-1.22) {$P_2$};
      \node[anchor=north,font=\small] at (0,-1.62)
        {Two slab directions};
    \end{scope}
    \begin{scope}[xshift=2.8cm]
      \foreach \k in {0,...,7} {
        \coordinate (p\k) at
          ({1.6*cos(22.5+45*\k)/cos(22.5)},
           {sin(22.5+45*\k)/cos(22.5)});
      }
      \filldraw[fill=slabfill,draw=slabblue,line width=0.8pt]
        (p0) \foreach \k in {1,...,7} {-- (p\k)} -- cycle;
      \filldraw[fill=white,draw=black,line width=0.6pt]
        (0,0) ellipse [x radius=1.6,y radius=1];
      \node at (0,0) {$E$};
      \node[anchor=north] at (0,-1.22) {$P_4$};
      \node[anchor=north,font=\small] at (0,-1.62)
```
